    Mandatory K-Dense skill usage:
    Use the installed K-Dense `exploratory-data-analysis` skill.

    /Users/emily/.agents/skills/exploratory-data-analysis/SKILL.md
    2. Append the Phase 2B usage record to:
    00_admin/SKILL_USAGE_LOG.tsv
    3. Do not perform subtype classification, differential expression,
    supervised feature selection, or biological interpretation.
    Inputs:
    - 03_processed/expression/GSE172356_expression_audited.tsv.gz

    - 01_metadata/expression_sample_crosswalk.tsv
    - 01_metadata/sample_manifest.tsv

    ## Task 1: audit the 73,202 missing values
    Determine:
    - whether missing entries are blank fields, literal NA values,
    parsing failures,
    or another source representation;
    - missing values per sample;
    - missing values per gene;
    - whether missingness is concentrated in specific genes or samples;
    - whether missingness is associated with total expression, subtype,
    batch,
    or the four suspected outliers;
    - whether the official source documentation explains the missing
    entries.
    Create:
    05_results/tables/phase2b_missingness_by_sample.tsv
    05_results/tables/phase2b_missingness_by_gene.tsv
    05_results/figures/phase2b_missingness_heatmap.pdf
    Do not replace missing values until their origin has been
    documented.
    ## Task 2: define missing-value handling
    Select a primary strategy based on source evidence.
    Possible strategies must be evaluated explicitly:
    - retain genes with complete observations;
    - remove genes exceeding a prespecified missingness threshold;
    - replace structurally absent count-like entries with zero only if
    supported
    by the source-file semantics;
    - use another justified approach.
    Run sensitivity analyses with at least two reasonable missing-value
    strategies.
    Record the selected strategy and rejected alternatives in:
    09_docs/planning/DECISION_LOG.md
    ## Task 3: expression filtering
    Evaluate filtering without using subtype labels.
    Report the number of genes retained under multiple candidate rules,
    such as:
    - removal of all-zero genes;
    - minimum non-missing prevalence;
    - minimum expression in a prespecified number or proportion of
    samples;
    - low-variance filtering only for unsupervised visualization, not
    for all
    downstream biological analyses.
    Do not select a threshold because it gives better subtype
    separation.
    Create:
    05_results/tables/phase2b_filtering_sensitivity.tsv
    ## Task 4: transformation
    The source matrix is DESeq size-factor-normalized counts.
    Do not perform another library-size or size-factor normalization.
    Evaluate an appropriate transformation for unsupervised analysis,
    primarily:
    log2(normalized_count + 1)
    Document why DESeq2 VST or rlog cannot automatically be applied to
    an already
    normalized, potentially non-integer matrix unless raw counts are
    available.
    Preserve both:
    1. filtered normalized-count matrix;
    2. filtered log2-transformed matrix.
    Create:
    03_processed/expression/GSE172356_expression_filtered_normalized.tsv.gz
    03_processed/expression/GSE172356_expression_log2_analysis_ready.tsv.gz
    ## Task 5: reassess the four suspected outliers
    Evaluate:
    - sample missingness;
    - total expression;
    - detected genes;
    - median sample correlation;
    - PCA distance;
    - robust Mahalanobis distance where applicable;
    - metadata or batch differences.
    Do not remove any sample based on a single metric.
    Create:
    05_results/tables/phase2b_outlier_assessment.tsv
    05_results/figures/phase2b_transformed_pca.pdf
    05_results/figures/phase2b_transformed_sample_correlation.pdf
    05_results/figures/phase2b_sample_qc_summary.pdf
    Classify each sample as:
    - RETAIN
    - RETAIN_WITH_SENSITIVITY_ANALYSIS
    - EXCLUDE_RECOMMENDED
    - TO_VERIFY
    Any exclusion recommendation must include objective evidence and a
    sensitivity
    analysis plan.
    ## Task 6: validation
    Verify:
    - exactly 62 mapped samples remain unless an exclusion is explicitly
    approved;
    - sample order matches expression_sample_crosswalk.tsv;
    - no duplicated samples or genes;
    - no infinite values;
    - final missing-value count is documented;
    - transformation is reproducible;
    - original audited matrix remains unchanged.
    Create:
    06_scripts/python/04_phase2b_prepare_expression.py
    06_scripts/python/04_validate_phase2b_expression.py
    Run both scripts.

    ## Task 4: agreement analysis

    prespecified;
    - additionally examine where hybrid samples fall on the
    basal–classical axis.

    Perform Phase 4A: prespecify and lock the statistical framework for

    Primary questions

1.  Does the transcriptomic structure support three stable clusters?

2.  Is K=3 better supported than K=2, K=4, or other reasonable values?

3.  Are basal-like and classical samples more stable than hybrid
    samples?

4.  Does hybrid form a reproducible cluster, or does it represent an
    intermediate or heterogeneous state?

5.  How sensitive are cluster assignments to expression scale, gene
    filtering, sample exclusion, and resampling?
    Required method hierarchy
    Primary analysis
    Reproduce the locked original clustering framework using the
    verified original signature and its required preprocessing.
    Evaluate candidate cluster numbers:

-   K=2

-   K=3

-   K=4

-   K=5

-   K=6
    Do not use the public subtype labels to construct clusters or select
    K.
    Public labels may only be compared with clusters after unsupervised
    clustering has been completed.
    Secondary analysis
    Evaluate an independent unsupervised representation using genes
    selected without subtype labels, such as prespecified high-variance
    gene sets.
    The exact gene-selection rule and number of genes must be fixed
    before seeing cluster agreement.
    Independent biological-axis comparison
    Use Moffitt and PurIST only to characterize basal–classical
    positioning.
    Do not invent a hybrid category for binary classifiers.
    Stability statistics to prespecify
    Include:

-   consensus clustering;

-   consensus cumulative distribution function;

-   proportion of ambiguous clustering;

-   within-cluster consensus;

-   sample-level item consensus;

-   silhouette width;

-   bootstrap cluster Jaccard stability;

-   prediction strength where feasible;

-   adjusted Rand index across resampling runs;

-   sample assignment entropy;

-   co-clustering probability;

-   cluster size and minimum viable cluster size.
    Specify:

-   distance metric;

-   clustering algorithm;

-   resampling fraction;

-   number of resampling iterations;

-   feature-resampling policy;

-   random-seed policy;

-   rules for aligning arbitrary cluster numbers to reference labels
    after clustering.
    Use Hungarian matching or another prespecified label-alignment
    method only after clustering.
    Required sensitivity analyses
    Prespecify analyses for:

1.  all 62 samples;

2.  exclusion of:

    -   YX16135T

    -   YX16158T

    -   YX16194T

    -   YX16224T;

1.  normalized-count scale under the original method;

2.  log2-transformed scale;

3.  gene-wise centering or z-scoring only where methodologically
    appropriate;

4.  alternative Phase 2B missing-value handling;

5.  reasonable variation in gene-filtering thresholds;

6.  sample resampling;

7.  feature resampling.
    Do not select the preprocessing strategy according to which version
    best reproduces the public labels.
    Sample-level hybrid assessment
    Predefine metrics for each sample:

-   probability of assignment to each cluster;

-   item consensus;

-   bootstrap assignment frequency;

-   assignment entropy;

-   silhouette width;

-   basal score;

-   classical score;

-   distance to basal and classical centroids.
    Predefine possible interpretations:

-   STABLE_BASAL

-   STABLE_CLASSICAL

-   STABLE_HYBRID

-   INTERMEDIATE_STATE

-   HETEROGENEOUS_OR_UNSTABLE

-   METHOD_SENSITIVE

-   TO_VERIFY
    Do not assign these interpretations during Phase 4A.
    Decision rules
    Define objective rules for conclusions such as:

1.  strong support for three discrete clusters;

2.  partial support for three clusters;

3.  stronger support for two major poles with an intermediate group;

4.  no sufficiently stable discrete cluster structure.
    The decision must not rely on a single statistic.
    Explicitly state how the following Phase 3B result affects the
    design:

-   exact reproduction on the required input scale;

-   26 changed assignments under the log2 stress test;

-   exact agreement of only 0.581 under that stress test.
    Treat this as evidence of preprocessing sensitivity, not as a
    replacement for the locked primary method.

    Document whether the matrix contains:
    - raw classified reads;
    - Bracken-estimated counts;
    - relative abundances;
    - normalized abundances;
    - another data type.

    Do not reconstruct pseudo-counts from relative abundance and
    describe them
    as raw sequencing counts.
    ## Task 2: prevalence and abundance filtering
    Prespecify one primary rule and multiple sensitivity rules.
    Evaluate candidate prevalence thresholds such as:
    - detected in at least 10% of samples;
    - detected in at least 20% of samples;
    - detected in at least 30% of samples.
    Define “detected” using the documented abundance scale.
    Also define an abundance criterion appropriate to the source values.
    Do not choose the rule based on subtype separation, P values,
    ordination,
    or downstream model performance.

    - genera retained;
    - genera removed;
    - matrix zero fraction;

    ## Task 3: zero and pseudocount policy
    Define the primary policy for zero-containing compositional data.

    - avoidance of arbitrary large pseudocounts.
    The pseudocount must be defined relative to the data scale and
    locked before
    association analysis.

    ## Task 4: compositional representations
    Define the project roles of:
    Primary:
    - CLR-transformed abundance;
    - Aitchison distance.
    Secondary or sensitivity:
    - robust CLR;
    - prevalence-based presence/absence;
    - Bray–Curtis only as a descriptive comparison;
    - count-based models only if valid raw or estimated counts are
    available.
    Do not use ordinary Euclidean distance on untransformed relative
    abundance
    as the primary microbiome distance.
    ## Task 5: contamination-sensitivity framework
    No sequenced negative controls are available.
    Therefore:
    - do not prescribe decontam prevalence or frequency analysis as
    completed;
    - do not automatically remove all environmental genera;
    - create evidence-based contamination flag categories;
    - define analyses with and without flagged genera;
    - define leave-one-genus-out checks for major findings;
    - evaluate associations with library size or total microbial
    abundance;
    - record database and literature sources for contamination flags.
    Allowed contamination categories:
    - HIGH_RISK_POTENTIAL_CONTAMINANT
    - MODERATE_RISK_ENVIRONMENTAL
    - BIOLOGICALLY_PLAUSIBLE_BUT_CONTAMINATION_SENSITIVE
    - LOW_CURRENT_CONCERN
    - TO_VERIFY
    Contamination flags must not be treated as proof of contamination.

    Before starting:
    1. Locate and read both exact SKILL.md files.

    - 04_analysis/04_microbiome_qc/PHASE6B_MICROBIOME_METHOD_LOCK.md
    - 04_analysis/04_microbiome_qc/PHASE6C_ANALYSIS_READY_MICROBIOME.md

    5. Do not reinterpret the public three-class labels as independently

    ## Primary scientific question
    Determine whether genus-level tumor microbiome composition is
    associated
    with the continuous basal–classical transcriptional state in PDAC.
    ## Outcome hierarchy
    Lock the following hierarchy:
    PRIMARY HOST OUTCOME:
    - Moffitt50 basal–classical contrast
    SECONDARY HOST OUTCOME:
    - basal/classical coactivation score
    SENSITIVITY HOST OUTCOMES:
    - Moffitt49 no-LEMD1 contrast
    - singscore basal–classical contrast
    - PurIST basal probability
    - Phase 4B assignment entropy
    DESCRIPTIVE OUTCOME ONLY:
    - public Basal / Hybrid / Classical labels
    Do not optimize outcomes or thresholds according to microbiome
    results.
    ## Task 1: global community-level tests
    Prespecify global association testing using:
    - primary Aitchison distance matrix;
    - PERMANOVA with 9,999 permutations;
    - continuous Moffitt50 contrast as the primary predictor;
    - PERMDISP/betadisper for categorical analyses;
    - marginal and sequential sums of squares clearly distinguished;
    - effect size reported as R-squared.
    Public subtype PERMANOVA is descriptive only.
    Do not select preprocessing according to the smallest PERMANOVA P
    value.
    ## Task 2: genus-level primary model
    For each of the 122 primary retained genera, prespecify:
    Primary model:
    CLR_genus ~ standardized_Moffitt50_contrast
    Use:
    - ordinary least squares;
    - HC3 heteroscedasticity-robust standard errors;
    - standardized host score;
    - coefficient interpreted as relative CLR change per one-SD increase
    in
    basal–classical contrast.
    The compositional relative nature of CLR coefficients must be stated
    explicitly.
    Primary multiple-testing family:
    - 122 genus tests for Moffitt50 contrast;
    - Benjamini–Hochberg FDR;
    - primary threshold q < 0.05;
    - also report raw P values, q values, coefficients, confidence
    intervals
    and standardized effect sizes.
    ## Task 3: supporting association methods
    Prespecify supporting analyses:
    - Spearman correlation;
    - permutation-based association test;
    - bootstrap confidence interval;
    - MaAsLin2 on CLR values with:
    normalization = "NONE"
    transform = "NONE"
    MaAsLin2 must be treated as supporting or sensitivity evidence
    rather than
    an independent replication of the same matrix.
    ## Task 4: model hierarchy and covariates
    Avoid overfitting with n=62.
    Lock the following model hierarchy:
    Model 0 — primary:
    - host transcriptional score only
    Model 1 — technical sensitivity:
    - host score
    - matrix total-abundance proxy
    Clearly state that the proxy is not an absolute microbial-load
    measurement.
    Model 2 — clinical sensitivity:
    - host score
    - age
    - sex
    - stage
    Run Model 2 only if:
    - at least 50 patients have complete data;
    - each categorical level contains at least 5 patients;
    - collinearity diagnostics are acceptable.
    Do not collapse clinical categories after inspecting association
    results.
    Tumor purity, immune and stromal scores should be marked as deferred
    covariates if they have not yet been calculated and validated.
    ## Task 5: contamination sensitivity
    Primary analysis retains all 122 genera.
    Prespecify sensitivity analyses:
    1. excluding HIGH_RISK_POTENTIAL_CONTAMINANT genera;
    2. excluding HIGH_RISK and MODERATE_RISK genera;
    3. leave-one-genus-out checks for top findings;
    4. association adjustment for matrix total-abundance proxy;
    5. flag-specific reporting for biologically plausible but
    contamination-sensitive genera.
    Do not label any genus as confirmed contamination.
    ## Task 6: preprocessing sensitivity
    Prespecify reruns using:
    - 10% prevalence matrix;
    - 30% prevalence matrix;
    - abundance >10 matrix;
    - pseudocount 0.1;
    - pseudocount 1.0;
    - rCLR;
    - matrices excluding three technical extreme samples;
    - high-risk contaminant exclusion;
    - high- plus moderate-risk exclusion.
    For every candidate association report:
    - sign consistency;
    - effect-size range;
    - q-value range;
    - number and fraction of sensitivity analyses with the same
    direction;
    - whether statistical support depends on one preprocessing choice.
    ## Task 7: presence/absence sensitivity
    For sufficiently prevalent genera, define an additional binary
    model.
    Possible approaches:
    - host score ~ genus presence/absence;
    - or genus presence/absence ~ standardized host score using logistic
    regression.
    Select one orientation and lock it before execution.
    Require minimum numbers of present and absent samples to avoid
    separation.
    Do not apply presence/absence models to rare genera.
    ## Task 8: evidence classification
    Predefine internal evidence categories.
    ROBUST_ASSOCIATION:
    - primary BH q < 0.05;
    - bootstrap confidence interval excludes zero;
    - same effect direction in Spearman analysis;
    - same direction in at least 75% of locked preprocessing
    sensitivities;
    - not eliminated solely by removal of one technical sample;
    - contamination status explicitly considered.
    SUGGESTIVE_ASSOCIATION:
    - q between 0.05 and 0.10, or
    - q < 0.05 but limited sensitivity support.
    METHOD_SENSITIVE:
    - sign reversal or major rank change across reasonable preprocessing
    alternatives.
    CONTAMINATION_SENSITIVE:
    - signal disappears after exclusion of high-risk taxa or is
    dominated by a
    flagged environmental genus.
    NO_SUPPORTED_ASSOCIATION:
    - insufficient statistical and robustness evidence.
    TO_VERIFY:
    - incomplete metadata or implementation uncertainty.
    These are internal evidence categories, not external validation
    levels.
    ## Task 9: secondary outcomes
    For coactivation, PurIST probability, Moffitt49, singscore and
    entropy:
    - run separate hypothesis families;
    - apply BH FDR within each outcome;
    - clearly mark them as secondary or sensitivity analyses;
    - do not combine their P values as independent replication;
    - require directionally coherent interpretation.
    ## Task 10: descriptive discrete subtype analyses
    For public Basal / Hybrid / Classical labels, prespecify:
    - descriptive Aitchison PERMANOVA;
    - PERMDISP/betadisper;
    - genus-level Kruskal–Wallis tests;
    - effect sizes;
    - post hoc pairwise tests only after an overall test;
    - BH correction.
    These results must remain secondary and descriptive.
    ## Task 11: sample-level influence
    Prespecify:
    - Cook's distance;
    - DFBETAs;
    - leave-one-sample-out analysis;
    - exclusion sensitivity for BasalLike1, Hybrid18 and Hybrid23;
    - reporting of findings driven by one or two patients.
    Do not automatically remove influential samples.
    ## Task 12: negative and null results
    Require reporting of:
    - no global community association;
    - no genera meeting primary FDR;
    - inconsistent directions across transformations;
    - associations lost after contamination sensitivity analysis.
    Do not promote nominal P values as discoveries when primary FDR is
    not met.

    Candidate hierarchy
    PRIMARY TAXA:
    Use only the nine Phase 7C-verified ROBUST_ASSOCIATION genera:

-   Azoarcus

-   Candida

-   Ensifer

-   Cutibacterium

-   Chryseobacterium

-   Ochrobactrum

-   Burkholderia

-   Rhizobium

-   Herbaspirillum
    SECONDARY TAXA:

-   the two verified SUGGESTIVE_ASSOCIATION genera
    Do not promote method-sensitive or contamination-sensitive genera
    into the primary mechanism analysis.
    Transformation-sensitivity requirement
    Eight of the nine robust genera showed direction reversal under the
    locked rCLR sensitivity representation.
    Therefore:

1.  Preserve the locked robust classification.

2.  Add a separate flag: RCLR_DIRECTION_SENSITIVE

3.  Do not describe these genera as directionally invariant across
    all compositional transformations.

4.  Require all downstream mechanism results to report both primary CLR
    and rCLR directions where the taxon is available.

5.  Treat a host-mechanism link as transformation-sensitive when
    its biological interpretation reverses under rCLR.
    Primary scientific questions

1.  Which host pathways and transcription-factor activities are
    associated with the nine verified taxa?

2.  Which host programs overlap with the basal–classical transcriptional
    axis?

3.  Are these associations explained by tumor purity, immune
    infiltration, or stromal content?

4.  Are candidate host mechanisms shared across several taxa or specific
    to a single genus?

5.  Which mechanisms remain stable under microbiome
    preprocessing, contamination, covariate, and sample-influence
    sensitivity analyses?
    Analysis hierarchy
    Layer 1 — predefined pathway activity
    Primary pathway collections:

-   MSigDB Hallmark

-   PROGENy pathway activities
    Secondary collections:

-   Reactome

-   KEGG, where gene-set interpretation is appropriate
    Do not screen an unlimited number of overlapping pathway databases
    and select only favorable results.
    Layer 2 — transcription-factor activity
    Use a verified transcription-factor activity framework such as:

-   DoRothEA/VIPER
    Lock:

-   regulon confidence levels;

-   minimum target-gene coverage;

-   input expression scale;

-   activity-score direction;

-   multiple-testing family.
    Layer 3 — tumor microenvironment programs
    Use validated host-derived scores:

-   inferred tumor purity

-   immune score

-   stromal score

-   ESTIMATE score
    Additional immune deconvolution methods may be considered only as
    secondary analyses and must be verified before execution.
    Layer 4 — data-driven co-expression modules
    Use a prespecified WGCNA or comparable module framework.
    Lock before execution:

-   gene filtering;

-   transformation;

-   soft-threshold selection rule;

-   minimum module size;

-   module merging threshold;

-   module preservation and robustness checks.
    Public subtype labels and microbiome association results must not be
    used to select network parameters.
    Layer 5 — taxon-associated host genes
    Treat genome-wide host-gene association as exploratory and
    high-dimensional.
    For each primary taxon, prespecify:
    host_gene_expression ~ standardized_CLR_genus
    Possible sensitivity covariates:

-   inferred tumor purity

-   immune score

-   stromal score
    Use:

-   appropriate linear modeling;

-   empirical Bayes moderation where valid;

-   BH correction separately for each taxon;

-   effect sizes and confidence intervals.
    Do not combine all taxa and genes into one undifferentiated
    multiple-testing family without explicitly documenting the
    hierarchy.
    Circularity safeguards
    The Moffitt50 score is derived from the same expression matrix used
    for host mechanism analysis.
    Therefore:

1.  Do not describe correlations with Moffitt signature genes as
    independent mechanistic validation.

2.  Report analyses both:

    -   including all host genes/pathways;

    -   excluding the Moffitt50 signature genes where technically
        applicable.

1.  Distinguish:

    -   direct reuse of subtype-signature genes;

    -   independent pathway or TF evidence;

    -   immune/stromal composition effects.

1.  Do not construct pathway gene sets from Phase 7B-associated genes
    and then test the same data without identifying the analysis as
    exploratory.
    Primary statistical model
    For every predefined pathway or TF activity:
    host_activity ~ standardized_CLR_genus
    Use:

-   one model per primary taxon;

-   HC3 robust standard errors or another locked valid method;

-   effect size and 95% confidence interval;

-   BH FDR within each predefined pathway family;

-   two-sided tests.
    Sensitivity models:

-    

    -   inferred tumor purity

-    

    -   immune score

-    

    -   stromal score

-   rCLR taxon representation

-   removal of the three technical microbiome extreme samples

-   contaminant-exclusion representation

-   leave-one-sample-out analysis
    Do not place inferred purity, immune score, stromal score, and
    ESTIMATE score together in one model.
    Multi-taxon shared-mechanism analysis
    Prespecify a framework to distinguish:

-   mechanisms associated with one genus only;

-   mechanisms shared by multiple genera;

-   mechanisms whose apparent sharing reflects correlated compositional
    taxa.
    Possible methods:

-   cross-taxon sign-consistency summary;

-   multivariable taxon model only if collinearity and sample-size
    criteria are satisfied;

-   sparse multivariate methods only as exploratory analyses.
    Do not interpret correlated taxa as independent biological
    exposures.
    Evidence categories
    Predefine host-mechanism evidence categories:
    ROBUST_HOST_MECHANISM:

-   pathway or TF activity passes locked FDR;

-   confidence interval excludes zero;

-   direction is stable under the required sensitivity analyses;

-   not driven by one sample;

-   not explained solely by purity, immune, or stromal adjustment;

-   rCLR transformation does not reverse the biological interpretation.
    TRANSFORMATION_SENSITIVE_MECHANISM:

-   primary CLR association is supported but rCLR reverses or removes
    the biological interpretation.
    COMPOSITION_SENSITIVE_MECHANISM:

-   association is substantially attenuated after purity, immune, or
    stromal adjustment.
    SAMPLE_SENSITIVE_MECHANISM:

-   direction or support depends on one or two patients.
    EXPLORATORY_HOST_MECHANISM:

-   nominal or secondary evidence without full robustness.
    NO_SUPPORTED_MECHANISM:

-   insufficient evidence after correction and sensitivity analysis.
    TO_VERIFY:

-   incomplete implementation or metadata.
    Mechanistic interpretation limits
    Require the final report to use:

-   “associated with”

-   “consistent with”

-   “suggests a potential link”
    Do not use:

-   “drives”

-   “causes”

-   “mediates”
    Causal mediation analysis is not permitted in this phase.
    Literature use
    Use citation-management only after computational candidates are
    defined.
    For each candidate mechanism, distinguish:

-   evidence generated in this project;

-   published PDAC evidence;

-   published evidence from other cancers;

-   microbial functional speculation.
    Do not use literature to rescue unsupported computational findings.
    Do not cite or discuss any user-provided draft manuscript.

    `00_admin/SKILL_USAGE_LOG.tsv`

    -

    4. Preserve:
    - Phase 4B discrete-cluster conclusion: `INCONCLUSIVE`
    - Phase 5B continuous-axis conclusion: `INCONCLUSIVE`
    - Phase 7C review decision: `PASS`
    - Phase 8C review decision: `PASS`
    5. Do not reinterpret the public Basal / Hybrid / Classical labels
    as
    independently validated discrete biological subtypes.
    6. Do not cite or discuss any user-provided draft or unpublished
    manuscript.
    ## Discovery findings to be externally evaluated
    ### Host transcriptional state
    Primary continuous host phenotype:
    - Moffitt50 basal–classical contrast
    Sensitivity host phenotypes:
    - Moffitt49 no-LEMD1 contrast
    - singscore basal–classical contrast
    - PurIST basal probability
    - basal/classical coactivation score
    - discrete-assignment entropy
    ### Microbiome finding
    The only taxon with Phase 8C-verified robust host-mechanism
    associations:
    - Ochrobactrum
    Important limitation:
    - Ochrobactrum is environmentally and contamination sensitive.
    - It must not be described as a confirmed tumor-resident organism.
    - External validation must distinguish biological replication from
    technical contamination or reagent-associated signal.
    ### Verified Ochrobactrum-associated host features
    Pathway layer:
    - HALLMARK_PROTEIN_SECRETION
    - HALLMARK_SPERMATOGENESIS
    Transcription-factor layer:
    - 34 verified TF activity associations listed in:
    `05_results/tables/phase8c_robust_mechanism_audit.tsv`
    WGCNA layer:
    - MEblack
    - MEblue
    - MEgreen
    - MEgreenyellow
    - MEpurple
    - MEred
    - MEtan
    Do not treat all 43 robust evidence rows as independent biological
    mechanisms.
    ## Primary Phase 9 objective
    Determine which discovery findings represent reproducible PDAC host
    biology,
    which are specific to the discovery cohort, and which remain
    inseparable from
    microbiome measurement or contamination uncertainty.
    ## Validation hierarchy
    Lock four separate validation layers.
    ### Layer 1 — independent bulk-transcriptome validation
    Primary goals:
    1. Validate the basal–classical continuous transcriptional axis.
    2. Validate the two supported Hallmark programs.
    3. Validate the 34 TF activity candidates.
    4. Validate the seven WGCNA-derived module signatures.
    5. Test whether these host programs remain associated after
    tumor-purity and
    immune/stromal adjustment where suitable metadata are available.
    6. Evaluate clinical outcome associations only as secondary
    validation.
    Potential repositories to audit:
    - GEO
    - ArrayExpress / BioStudies
    - TCGA-PAAD
    - ICGC
    - cBioPortal-linked cohorts
    - other independently published PDAC transcriptome cohorts
    Use only primary public repositories, original publications, and
    official
    supplementary files.
    Exclude:
    - GSE172356;
    - any cohort that contains overlapping patients or samples;
    - datasets without sufficient sample-level expression and metadata;
    - cell-line-only datasets for patient-level validation.
    ### Layer 2 — single-cell validation
    Primary goals:
    1. Determine the cellular source of the supported host pathways,
    TFs, and
    module genes.
    2. Test whether supported programs are expressed in:
    - malignant epithelial cells;
    - CAF populations;
    - endothelial cells;
    - myeloid cells;
    - lymphoid cells;
    - other stromal compartments.
    3. Evaluate basal–classical scoring specifically within malignant
    cells.
    4. Determine whether the apparent host mechanism is tumor-cell
    intrinsic or
    primarily reflects cell-composition variation.
    5. Test whether individual malignant cells show basal/classical
    coactivation,
    rather than inferring hybrid state solely from bulk mixtures.
    Potential repositories to audit:
    - GEO
    - SRA
    - CELLxGENE
    - TISCH or equivalent curated cancer single-cell resources
    - original study repositories and supplementary objects
    Do not use automatic cell annotations without reviewing marker
    support.
    ### Layer 3 — spatial-transcriptomic validation
    Primary goals:
    1. Map basal and classical programs spatially.
    2. localize supported pathway, TF, and WGCNA-module activity.
    3. Test spatial proximity to:
    - malignant epithelial regions;
    - CAF-rich regions;
    - immune-rich regions;
    - necrotic or ductal regions.
    4. Evaluate whether apparently shared programs arise from spatial
    co-localization rather than within-cell regulation.
    Spatial validation is optional if no suitable independent PDAC
    dataset is
    available.
    ### Layer 4 — independent microbiome validation
    Primary goals:
    1. Determine whether Ochrobactrum is detected in independent PDAC
    tissue
    microbiome datasets.
    2. Determine whether detection survives:
    - host-read depletion;
    - low-biomass filtering;
    - negative-control comparison where controls exist;
    - prevalence and abundance filtering;
    - alternative taxonomic profilers;
    - batch and reagent-lot assessment.
    3. Determine whether Ochrobactrum abundance or presence is
    associated with
    the same host programs.
    Potential repositories:
    - SRA
    - ENA
    - BioProject
    - MG-RAST where appropriate
    - original PDAC tissue-microbiome studies
    Do not use stool, oral, pancreatic-fluid, or cell-line microbiomes
    as direct
    tumor-tissue replication cohorts. They may only serve as contextual
    evidence.
    TCGA-derived microbial profiles must be treated as exploratory, not
    primary
    validation.
    ## Task 1: authoritative dataset search
    Search public repositories and original literature for independent
    datasets
    covering:
    - PDAC bulk RNA-seq or microarray;
    - PDAC single-cell RNA-seq;
    - PDAC spatial transcriptomics;
    - PDAC tumor-tissue microbiome;
    - paired host transcriptome and microbiome, if any.
    For every candidate dataset verify:
    - accession;
    - publication;
    - organism;
    - tissue;
    - sample type;
    - number of PDAC tumors;
    - control samples;
    - platform;
    - raw and processed-data availability;
    - sample-level metadata;
    - survival or treatment metadata;
    - tumor-purity information;
    - overlap with the discovery cohort;
    - suitability for each validation layer.
    Do not assume that a dataset is suitable from its title alone.
    ## Task 2: create external-dataset inventory
    Create:
    `01_metadata/external_validation_dataset_inventory.tsv`
    Required columns:
    - dataset_id
    - accession
    - repository
    - publication
    - PMID_or_DOI
    - organism
    - disease
    - data_modality
    - platform
    - tissue_or_sample_type
    - tumor_samples
    - control_samples
    - processed_data_available
    - raw_data_available
    - clinical_metadata_available
    - survival_available
    - treatment_available
    - purity_metadata_available
    - cell_level_metadata_available
    - spatial_coordinates_available
    - negative_controls_available
    - microbiome_taxonomic_level
    - paired_host_microbiome
    - possible_sample_overlap
    - intended_validation_layer
    - suitability_status
    - exclusion_reason
    - official_source
    - access_date
    - notes
    Allowed `suitability_status` values:
    - PRIORITY_1
    - PRIORITY_2
    - EXPLORATORY_ONLY
    - NOT_SUITABLE
    - TO_VERIFY
    ## Task 3: define cohort inclusion criteria
    Prespecify minimum requirements separately for:
    ### Bulk transcriptome
    Include only cohorts with:
    - independent PDAC tumor samples;
    - usable sample-level gene-expression data;
    - sufficient gene coverage for the locked signatures;
    - identifiable sample columns;
    - at least 30 tumors for quantitative validation where possible.
    Smaller cohorts may be retained as secondary evidence but must be
    labelled
    underpowered.
    ### Single-cell
    Include only datasets with:
    - primary human PDAC tumors;
    - identifiable patient origin;
    - malignant-cell annotations that can be independently reviewed;
    - sufficient malignant cells and patients;
    - expression objects or matrices available.
    Avoid treating thousands of cells from a few patients as thousands
    of
    independent biological replicates.
    ### Spatial
    Include only datasets with:
    - primary human PDAC tissue;
    - sample-level patient identity;
    - expression matrix and spatial coordinates;
    - interpretable tissue regions.
    ### Microbiome
    Prefer datasets with:
    - PDAC tumor tissue;
    - raw shotgun-metagenomic or other sequence data;
    - sequencing negative controls;
    - extraction or reagent controls;
    - sample-level patient metadata;
    - host-transcriptome pairing where available.
    If negative controls are absent, classify the dataset as
    contamination
    limited.
    ## Task 4: signature transfer and harmonization plan
    For every bulk or single-cell dataset define:
    - gene identifier conversion;
    - duplicate-gene handling;
    - missing-signature-gene policy;
    - expression-scale requirements;
    - within-dataset normalization requirements;
    - batch handling;
    - pathway scoring method;
    - TF activity method;
    - WGCNA-module transfer method.
    Do not merge expression matrices across platforms before first
    performing
    within-cohort validation.
    Primary cross-cohort host validation should use locked signatures
    rather than
    retraining classifiers.
    ## Task 5: bulk-validation endpoints
    Prespecify the following endpoints.
    ### Basal–classical axis
    For each cohort calculate:
    - Moffitt50 basal score;
    - Moffitt50 classical score;
    - basal–classical contrast;
    - Moffitt49 no-LEMD1 sensitivity score;
    - PurIST probability where technically valid.
    Evaluate:
    - score distribution;
    - rank stability;
    - cohort-specific modality;
    - agreement among scoring methods;
    - association with published subtype labels where available.
    Do not optimize a cutoff against published labels.
    ### Host mechanism validation
    For each supported pathway, TF, and transferred WGCNA module
    evaluate:
    - score availability;
    - effect direction relative to the basal–classical axis;
    - association with tumor purity;
    - immune/stromal sensitivity;
    - cohort-level effect size and confidence interval.
    ### Clinical validation
    Where reliable survival or treatment metadata exist:
    - test host scores as secondary prognostic or response-associated
    variables;
    - report Cox proportional-hazards assumptions;
    - avoid data-driven cutpoint optimization;
    - use continuous scores as primary;
    - treat clinical findings as secondary to molecular replication.
    ## Task 6: WGCNA-module transfer strategy
    The discovery WGCNA module colors are project-specific labels.
    Therefore define transferred module signatures using:
    - complete module gene lists;
    - module eigengene-like scores;
    - standardized mean or rank-based module scores;
    - module preservation statistics where raw expression permits.
    Do not assume that an external dataset will reproduce the same
    module color
    or exact network topology.
    For each of the seven supported modules specify:
    - discovery genes;
    - genes available externally;
    - coverage threshold;
    - scoring method;
    - preservation method;
    - failure criteria.
    ## Task 7: single-cell validation statistics
    Prespecify patient-aware analysis.
    Use:
    - per-patient pseudobulk;
    - mixed-effects models where justified;
    - patient as the biological replicate;
    - malignant-cell-specific basal/classical scores;
    - cell-type-specific pathway and TF scores.
    Evaluate:
    - malignant-cell enrichment;
    - CAF, immune, and endothelial contributions;
    - patient-level reproducibility;
    - within-patient cellular heterogeneity;
    - potential bulk-composition explanation.
    Do not perform cell-level tests that treat all cells as independent
    patients.
    ## Task 8: spatial-validation statistics
    Prespecify:
    - spot or cell-level score calculation;
    - patient-level aggregation;
    - spatial autocorrelation-aware interpretation;
    - tumor-region versus stromal-region comparisons;
    - spatial co-localization statistics where supported;
    - correction for multiple regions within the same patient.
    Do not infer microbial spatial localization from host spatial
    transcriptomics
    unless microbial reads were directly measured.
    ## Task 9: microbiome-validation framework
    For independent tumor-tissue microbiome datasets prespecify:
    - raw-read QC;
    - host-read removal;
    - taxonomic profiling;
    - database version;
    - Ochrobactrum species/genus resolution;
    - prevalence;
    - abundance;
    - sequencing-negative-control comparison;
    - reagent-control comparison;
    - sample-batch association;
    - extraction-lot association;
    - low-biomass sensitivity;
    - alternative classifier sensitivity.
    Primary microbiome validation evidence must distinguish:
    - DETECTED_WITH_CONTROL_SUPPORT
    - DETECTED_BUT_CONTAMINATION_LIMITED
    - NOT_DETECTED_WITH_ADEQUATE_DEPTH
    - INSUFFICIENT_DATA
    - TO_VERIFY
    Do not count database-only or literature mentions as external
    replication.
    ## Task 10: paired host–microbiome validation
    If an independent paired cohort is found, prespecify:
    host_feature ~ standardized microbiome feature
    Use:
    - compositional representation appropriate to that dataset;
    - independently recalculated pseudocount or zero-replacement policy;
    - patient-level models;
    - purity, immune, or stromal sensitivity where available;
    - contamination-control sensitivity;
    - locked direction from the discovery cohort.
    Do not transfer the discovery cohort pseudocount unchanged.
    Do not refit or select a new host mechanism according to the
    external result.
    ## Task 11: cross-cohort evidence synthesis
    Prespecify:
    - cohort-specific effect sizes;
    - direction;
    - confidence intervals;
    - standardized scoring;
    - random-effects meta-analysis when at least three sufficiently
    comparable
    cohorts exist;
    - heterogeneity statistics;
    - leave-one-cohort-out sensitivity;
    - no pooling where platforms or biological sources are incompatible.
    Do not combine bulk, single-cell, spatial, and microbiome evidence
    into one
    P value.
    ## Task 12: external-validation evidence categories
    Define:
    ### EXTERNALLY_REPLICATED_HOST_FEATURE
    - same host feature available in an independent cohort;
    - direction consistent with discovery;
    - confidence interval excludes zero or prespecified replication test
    passes;
    - not dependent on one external cohort;
    - suitable gene coverage.
    ### PARTIALLY_REPLICATED_HOST_FEATURE
    - direction consistent but limited statistical support;
    - or replicated in one small cohort only.
    ### CELLULAR_SOURCE_SUPPORTED
    - single-cell or spatial evidence localizes the host feature to a
    plausible
    cell population or compartment.
    ### EXTERNALLY_REPLICATED_MICROBIOME_FEATURE
    - Ochrobactrum detected in independent tumor tissue;
    - adequate sequencing depth;
    - control-supported signal;
    - association direction consistent where paired host data exist.
    ### CONTAMINATION_LIMITED_MICROBIOME_EVIDENCE
    - detection present but negative-control or batch evidence is
    inadequate.
    ### NOT_REPLICATED
    - adequate independent data fail to reproduce the direction or
    signal.
    ### INSUFFICIENT_EXTERNAL_DATA
    - no technically suitable validation dataset.
    ### TO_VERIFY
    - unresolved implementation or metadata issue.
    Do not upgrade evidence using literature plausibility alone.
    ## Task 13: prioritization for Phase 9B execution
    Rank datasets separately for:
    - bulk host validation;
    - single-cell cellular-source validation;
    - spatial validation;
    - microbiome validation.
    For every PRIORITY_1 dataset specify:
    - exact accession;
    - exact files to download;
    - estimated download size;
    - required software;
    - expected runtime;
    - expected memory;
    - primary validation endpoints;
    - known limitations;
    - whether raw or processed data should be used first.
    Prefer processed matrices for initial host validation when
    provenance and
    processing are adequate.
    Do not download large FASTQ, BAM, or SRA files during Phase 9A.
    ## Task 14: negative-control and falsification analyses
    Prespecify negative controls such as:
    - unrelated Hallmark pathways;
    - randomized module gene sets matched by size and expression;
    - gene-label permutation;
    - patient-label permutation;
    - validation in nonmalignant pancreas where appropriate;
    - contamination-associated genera not supported in discovery.
    Negative controls must be clearly separated from primary validation.

    ## Task 10: cellular-source evaluation
    For every locked host feature evaluate:
    1. Which major cell type has the highest activity?
    2. Is the feature detectable in malignant cells?
    3. Is it primarily stromal, immune, endothelial, or epithelial?
    4. Is its patient-level variation reproducible?
    5. Does the bulk signal plausibly reflect cellular composition?
    6. Does the feature remain associated with malignant-cell Moffitt50
    contrast?
    Use patient-aware models.
    Possible primary models:
    patient_celltype_feature_score ~ cell_type + dataset
    For malignant-cell-specific analyses:
    malignant_feature_score ~ malignant_Moffitt50_contrast
    Use:
    - patient-level pseudobulk scores;
    - dataset-aware mixed or fixed-effects models where justified;
    - effect sizes and confidence intervals;
    - two-sided tests;
    - locked multiple-testing families.
    Do not use cell-level P values as patient-level evidence.
    Create:
    05_results/tables/phase9b2_cellular_source_models.tsv
    ## Task 11: malignant-cell basal–classical heterogeneity
    Within malignant cells assess:

    - within-patient score variance;

    - simultaneous basal and classical program activation;
    - whether apparent bulk Hybrid states can arise from:
    - intermediate malignant-cell states;
    - coactivated malignant cells;
    - mixtures of distinct malignant populations;
    - tumor–stroma composition.

    ## Interpretation boundary
    Phase 9B2 evaluates:
    - the cellular origin of host transcriptional programs;
    - malignant-cell specificity;
    - stromal or immune contributions;
    - patient-level malignant-state heterogeneity.
    Phase 9B2 does not validate:
    - Ochrobactrum presence;
    - microbial abundance;
    - microbial localization;
    - microbial causality;
    - microbe–cell physical interactions.
    Use the description:
    “single-cell cellular-source evaluation of bulk-externally
    replicated TF
    activities and partially replicated or discovery-supported host
    programs.”
    Do not describe all evaluated programs as fully externally
    replicated.

    Do not reconstruct new WGCNA modules from the single-cell cohort.
    ## Task 10: calculate TF activities
    Use the project-local renv environment.
    Calculate regulon-based activity using the locked DoRothEA/VIPER or
    decoupleR
    implementation.

    - regulon confidence levels A, B and C;
    - the locked minimum target coverage;
    - cohort-specific target coverage;
    - no TF-symbol expression proxies.

    expression.

    localization if

    ## Task 11: cellular-source evaluation
    For every locked host feature determine:
    1. whether it is detectable in malignant cells;
    2. which major cell type shows the highest patient-level activity;
    3. whether it is primarily epithelial, stromal, endothelial, or
    immune;
    4. whether patient-level variation is reproducible;
    5. whether its bulk signal could reflect cellular composition;
    6. whether it remains associated with malignant-cell Moffitt50
    contrast.
    Use patient-aware models.
    For cell-type localization, use an appropriate repeated-measures
    framework,
    for example:
    patient_celltype_feature_score ~ cell_type + (1 | patient_id)
    where model assumptions and sample counts permit.
    For malignant-cell analyses use:
    malignant_feature_score ~ malignant_Moffitt50_contrast
    Report:
    - eligible patients;
    - coefficient;
    - standard error;
    - confidence interval;
    - P value;
    - BH q value;
    - effect direction;
    - model diagnostics.
    Apply BH correction separately within:
    - Hallmark;
    - WGCNA-module;
    - externally replicated TF;
    - partially replicated TF
    feature families.
    Create:
    05_results/tables/phase9b2_cellular_source_models.tsv
    ## Task 12: malignant-cell basal–classical heterogeneity
    Within malignant cells assess:
    - patient-level basal–classical score distribution;
    - within-patient score variance;
    - coexistence of basal-like and classical-like cells;
    - simultaneous basal and classical program activation;
    - whether an apparent bulk intermediate or Hybrid state can arise
    from:
    - intermediate malignant cells;
    - coactivated malignant cells;
    - mixtures of distinct malignant populations;
    - tumor–stroma composition.
    Use continuous scores.
    Do not optimize a new single-cell Hybrid threshold.
    Do not claim that thousands of cells provide thousands of
    independent
    patients.

    05_results/tables/phase9b2_malignant_state_heterogeneity.tsv

    ## Issue 1: covariate and model-formula inconsistency
    The locked Hypothesis 3 states that the primary association should
    be
    adjusted for:
    - CAF fraction
    - myeloid fraction
    - lymphoid fraction
    However, the displayed primary model formula includes CAF and
    myeloid
    fractions but omits lymphoid fraction.
    Reconcile the hypothesis text, parameter inventory, protocol, method
    lock,
    and validator.
    The primary fully adjusted model should contain all prospectively
    required
    composition covariates when estimable:
    protein_secretion_score ~
    Moffitt50_contrast +
    CAF_fraction +
    myeloid_fraction +
    lymphoid_fraction +
    relevant_locked_technical_covariates +
    random_effects
    Do not automatically include covariates unavailable in a dataset.
    Define a prospectively locked reduced-model hierarchy for cases of:
    - unavailable covariates;
    - severe collinearity;
    - insufficient patient count;
    - model nonconvergence.
    Do not remove a covariate according to its P value.
    Record for each dataset:
    - full model;
    - reduced model;
    - reason a reduced model is permitted;
    - collinearity criterion;
    - minimum complete-patient requirement.
    ## Issue 2: patient, section, ROI, and spatial-unit nesting
    The current documents state that sections, ROIs, spots, or segmented
    cells
    are nested within patients, but the displayed model contains only:
    (1 | patient_id)
    Determine the actual hierarchy separately for:
    - HWANG_GSE202051_NAIVE
    - HWANG_GSE202051_TREATED
    - MONCADA_GSE111672
    For every dataset explicitly define:
    - patient_id
    - section_id
    - ROI_id, spot_id, or spatial_cell_id
    - number of sections per patient
    - number of ROIs or spots per section
    - whether more than one section exists per patient
    - biological replicate
    - inferential observation
    - descriptive observation
    Where multiple sections exist per patient and the data support it,
    use an
    appropriate nested structure such as:
    (1 | patient_id) + (1 | patient_id:section_id)
    or an equivalent patient-aware model.
    Where the dataset has only one section per patient, document why the
    section
    random effect is not identifiable.
    For GeoMx ROI-level analysis, ROI must not be treated as an
    independent
    patient.
    For spot-level spatial-transcriptomic analysis, spots must not be
    treated as
    independent biological replicates.
    Define patient-level aggregation or mixed-effects alternatives where
    required.
    Do not use the phrase “mixed-effects OLS.” Use the correct model
    class, such
    as:
    - linear mixed-effects model;
    - generalized linear mixed-effects model;
    - patient-level regression;
    - patient-level meta-analysis.

    ## Issue 1: Hwang ROI-level pairing
    The Hwang GeoMx data have the following verified structure:
    ### HWANG_GSE202051_NAIVE
    - 18 patients
    - 18 sections
    - 128 ROIs
    - 256 segments
    - each ROI segmented into one tumor compartment and one stromal
    compartment
    ### HWANG_GSE202051_TREATED
    - 25 patients
    - 25 sections
    - 176 ROIs
    - 352 segments
    - each ROI segmented into one tumor compartment and one stromal
    compartment
    The current model includes only:
    (1 | patient_id)
    This is insufficient for analyses that jointly include the paired
    tumor and
    stromal segments from the same ROI.
    Define separate model families according to the actual hypothesis.
    ### Model family A: compartment-localization models
    For analyses comparing tumor and stromal segments, use an ROI-aware
    model such
    as:
    feature_score ~
    compartment +
    Moffitt50_contrast +
    CAF_fraction +
    myeloid_fraction +
    lymphoid_fraction +
    relevant_locked_technical_covariates +
    (1 | patient_id) +
    (1 | patient_id:ROI_id)
    or the equivalent nested notation:
    (1 | patient_id/ROI_id)
    The exact formula may be simplified only through the prospectively
    locked
    reduced-model hierarchy.
    The ROI random effect must account for pairing between tumor and
    stromal
    segments originating from the same ROI.
    ### Model family B: malignant-segment axis models
    For analyses restricted to one malignant-enriched tumor segment per
    ROI, use:
    protein_secretion_score ~
    Moffitt50_contrast +
    CAF_fraction +
    myeloid_fraction +
    lymphoid_fraction +
    relevant_locked_technical_covariates +
    (1 | patient_id)
    Because only one analyzed tumor segment is retained from each ROI,
    an ROI
    random intercept may not be identifiable or necessary.
    Explicitly document the restriction to tumor segments.
    ### Model family C: tumor–stroma paired contrasts
    Where scientifically appropriate, calculate within-ROI paired
    contrasts:
    tumor_segment_score - stromal_segment_score
    Then perform patient-aware analysis of ROI-level contrasts using:
    paired_contrast ~ relevant_predictors + (1 | patient_id)
    Do not treat the 256 or 352 segments as independent biological
    replicates.
    ## Issue 2: explicit ROI identifiers
    Update all planning tables so that Hwang records contain separate
    fields for:
    - patient_id
    - section_id
    - ROI_id
    - segment_id
    - compartment

    - biological_replicate

    - patient_id is the biological replicate;
    - ROI_id is a paired technical/spatial sampling unit;
    - segment_id is the observed compartment-level unit;
    - tumor and stromal segments from one ROI are correlated
    observations.
    The validator must fail if ROI_id is missing from an analysis
    involving both
    tumor and stromal segments.
    ## Issue 3: Moncada low-patient-count limitation
    The verified Moncada structure is:
    - 2 patients
    - 6 sections
    - 4 sections for Patient A
    - 2 sections for Patient B
    - 2248 spots
    Two patients are insufficient for conventional population-level
    patient-aware
    replication inference.
    Reclassify the Moncada role from formal secondary replication to:
    EXPLORATORY_CROSS_PLATFORM_SPATIAL_CONSISTENCY
    or an equivalent explicit status.
    Do not remove the dataset.
    Do not allow Moncada alone to generate a formal population-level
    replication
    claim.
    ## Issue 4: Moncada analysis framework
    For Moncada, lock the following hierarchy:
    1. Perform section-specific spatial analyses.
    2. Use within-section coordinate or label permutations for
    significance.
    3. Summarize sections within each patient.
    4. Report whether the effect direction is consistent across sections
    within
    each patient.
    5. Report whether the direction is consistent in both patients.
    6. Compare the direction descriptively with the Hwang naïve primary
    cohort.
    Do not treat spots as independent biological replicates.
    Do not derive a patient-level random-effects variance from only two
    patients.
    Do not report a fixed-effects meta-analysis of two patient
    coefficients as
    population-level external replication.
    A section-level coefficient synthesis may be reported descriptively,
    but its
    P value must not be interpreted as evidence from six independent
    patients.
    Allowed Moncada evidence statements should be limited to:
    - directionally consistent in both patients;
    - directionally inconsistent;
    - supported by within-section spatial permutations;
    - not supported;
    - insufficient spatial data.
    ## Issue 5: cross-cohort evidence synthesis
    Lock the final hierarchy:
    ### Primary inferential evidence
    HWANG_GSE202051_NAIVE
    ### Treatment-sensitivity evidence
    HWANG_GSE202051_TREATED
    ### Exploratory cross-platform spatial consistency
    MONCADA_GSE111672
    Do not merge GeoMx segments and spatial-transcriptomic spots into
    one model.
    Do not calculate a conventional pooled effect across all three
    canonical
    cohorts.
    Define the spatial evidence synthesis as:
    - primary support from Hwang naïve;
    - direction and robustness assessment in Hwang treated;
    - exploratory directional and spatial consistency assessment in
    Moncada.
    A feature may receive formal spatial support only if the locked
    primary Hwang
    naïve criteria are satisfied.
    Moncada may strengthen interpretation but cannot rescue a failed
    primary
    result.
